Reliability of choice decoding from CDchoice A receiver operating curve (ROC)-AUC analysis was performed to esti- mate the reliability of choice decoding from projections along CDchoice  on a single-session basis (Extended Data Fig. 2c). For each session, equal  numbers of correct left and right lick trials were split into a training set  (70%) and a testing set (30%). CD choice was calculated from the activ - ity of the neural population using training trials as described above.  Activity from testing trials was projected along CDchoice and provided as  input to a logistic regression model (fitglm() in MATLAB with distribu- tion\\u2009=\\u2009\\u2018Binomial\\u2019 and link\\u2009=\\u2009\\u2018logit\\u2019). The model was optimized to predict  the animal\\u2019s choice on a particular trial from the delay epoch CD choice  activity. The model output was then passed into MATLAB\\u2019s perfcurve()  function to obtain an ROC. Reliability of decoding was measured as the  area under the ROC (AUC), shown in the inset of Extended Data Fig. 2c. Choice and context decoding from neural population or  kinematic features We trained logistic regression models to predict either animal choice  (Fig. 3b) or behavioral context (Fig. 4b) from either kinematic features  or single-trial neural activity. The kinematic regressors were made up  of the x and y positions and velocities of DeepLabCut-tracked features  (tongue, jaw and nose) as well as the tongue length, angle and motion  energy. The neural regressors were the firing rates of the units simulta- neously recorded within each session. Equal numbers of correct left and  right lick trials were used for this analysis. A separate model was trained  at each time bin for both neural and kinematic decoding. Models were  trained with ridge regularization and four-fold cross-validation with  30% of trials held out for testing. Chance accuracy was obtained by  shuffling choice/context labels across trials. Accuracy of the predic- tion was defined as: Accuracy = Numberofcorrectpredictions Totalnumberofpredictions Emergence of CDchoice selectivity and motion energy in  randomized versus fixed delay tasks T o find selectivity within the CD choice projection (Fig. 3h, top left),  we found the difference between the trial-averaged projection on  right and left trials for each session. We then found the maximum  selectivity value before the go cue and identified the first timepoint,  relative to delay onset, for the selectivity trace to exceed 90% of the  maximum value (Fig. 3h, top right). The same metric was found using  session-averaged motion energy in Fig. 3h, bottom panels. Predicting projections along CDs from kinematic features We trained support vector machines (SVMs) to predict projections  along CDchoice, CDramp and CDcontext. Correct DR trials were used for pre- dicting CDchoice and CDramp projections; correct DR and WC trials were  used for predicting CDcontext. For predicting CDcontext, the regressors were  made up of the x and y positions and velocities of DeepLabCut-tracked  features (multiple points on the tongue, jaw and nose taken from two  camera angles) as well as the tongue length, angle and motion energy  (totaling 60 regressors). Only the sample and delay epoch were con- sidered when predicting CD choice and CDramp projections; because of  this, any tongue-related metrics were not included as regressors in  these analyses (totaling 55 regressors). Projections and kinematic  features were first mean centered and scaled to unit variance before  input to the regression model. The previous B time bins of kinematic  data were used to predict the current bin\\u2019s neural data (B\\u2009=\\u20096; each bin  is 5\\u2009ms). Models were trained with ridge regularization and four-fold  cross-validation. The models were tested on held-out testing data (40%  of trials for CD choice and CDramp; tested on 30% of trials for CD context).   T o assess the prediction quality between projections and model   predictions, we calculated the variance explained as:\\n\\n---\\n\\nPAGE 18\\nNature Neuroscience Article https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-024-01859-1 R2 =1 \\u2212 \\u2211t( yt \\u2212 \\u0302yt) 2 \\u2211t( yt \\u2212 y ) 2 where yt is the value of the projection at time t; \\u0177t is the prediction; and  \\u0233 is the mean of the projection across all time. When predicting projections along CDchoice during the randomized  delay task, unregularized models were used (Fig. 3h). This was due to  the small numbers of trials for each delay length, which precluded the  partitioning of data into three separate sets (training, validation and  testing trials). Instead, models were trained with linear regression  and four-fold cross-validation and tested on held-out data from each  cross-validation fold. For assessing prediction quality between projec- tions and model predictions, trials with 1.2-s delay epochs were used. T o understand which groups of kinematic features were most  informative for predicting projections along CD choice  or CD context   (Extended Data Fig. 3), the absolute value of the beta coefficients for  each kinematic regressor in the trained model was first obtained. For  each feature group ( jaw, nose and motion energy), the average beta  coefficient across all regressors belonging to this kinematic feature  group (8\\u00d7B for jaw, 6\\u00d7B for nose and 1\\u00d7B for motion energy; for exam- ple, the x and y positions and velocities for one point on the jaw on each  camera totals eight jaw regressors for each time bin, B) was calculated.  The average beta coefficient for each feature group was then expressed  as a fraction of the total of all beta coefficients. Estimating dimensionality of neural data The dimensionality of neural data (Extended Data Fig. 6a) was esti - mated using Parallel Analysis63,64. Null distributions were created for  each eigenvalue by shuffling the time bins separately for each column  of a matrix X \\u2208 \\u211dB\\u00d7N , where B is the number of time bins, and N  is the  number of units. The remaining covariance structure is then due to  chance. We repeated this procedure 1,000 times to generate a null  distribution for each eigenvalue. The number of eigenvalues of the  unshuffled data that exceed the 95th percentile of the null distribution  was used as an upper bound estimate of dimensionality. Subspace identification (trial-averaged data) Delay and response subspaces were identified following the general  approach described in ref. 27 . For identifying subspaces using  trial-averaged data (Fig. 5a), neural activity was first trial averaged  separately for correct left and right trials. Activity across trial types  was then concatenated, to produce a matrix X \\u2208 \\u211d(B\\u00d7C)\\u00d7N, where B is the  number of time bins; C is the number of trial types; and N is the number  of units. Trial-averaged neural activity was soft normalized (normaliza- tion factor\\u2009=\\u20095) as described in ref. 27 and subsequently baseline sub- tracted (baseline: \\u22122.4\\u2009s to \\u22122.2\\u2009s before go cue, during the ITI). The  matrix, X, was further divided into two matrices, Xdelay and Xresponse. Xdelay  contained activity from [\\u22121, 0] seconds relative to the go cue, and   Xresponse contained activity from [0,1] seconds relative to the go cue.  Delay and response subspaces were then identified by solving the   following optimization problem: [\\u02c6Qdelay, \\u02c6Qresponse] = argmax[Qdelay ,Qresponse ] 1 2 Tr(QT delay Cdelay Qdelay ) \\u2211 ddelay i=1 \\u03c3delay (i) + 1 2 Tr(QT response Cresponse Qresponse ) \\u2211 dresponse i=1 \\u03c3response (i) subjectto QT delayQresponse =0, QT delayQdelay = I, QT responseQresponse = I where Cdelay and Cresponse are the covariances of Xdelay and Xresponse; \\u03c3delay  and \\u03c3response are the singular values of Cdelay and Cresponse; and ddelay and  dresponse are the dimensionality of the subspaces. This optimization  problem jointly identifies the subspaces that maximally contain the  variance in neural activity during the delay and response epochs. The  dimensionality of each space was chosen to be half the number of  simultaneously recorded neurons or 20, whichever was smaller. There- fore, the dimensionality of the full population was equal to the dimen- sionality of the delay or response subspaces for sessions containing  40 or fewer neurons. Optimization was performed using the Manopt65  toolbox for MATLAB. Subspace identification (single-trial data) For identifying subspaces using single-trial data (Fig. 5h), single-trial  neural activity was first binned in 5-ms intervals and smoothed with a  causal Gaussian kernel with a half width of 35\\u2009ms. Each trial\\u2019s activity  was subtracted by the average baseline activity across trials and scaled  by the s.d. across trials (baseline: \\u22122.4\\u2009s to \\u22122.2\\u2009s before go cue, during  the ITI). X moving \\u2208 \\u211dBp \\u00d7N  and X stationary  \\u2208 \\u211dBn \\u00d7N  were defined using the  single-trial neural activity when the animal was moving or stationary  (see \\u2018Motion energy\\u2019 subsection), respectively. Bn is the number of time  bins during which the animal was annotated as stationary, and Bp is the  number of timepoints annotated as moving. All correct and error trials  from DR and WC contexts were used unless specified otherwise. Once  the data were partitioned into moving and stationary timepoints,  subspace identification was carried out as described for trial-averaged  data in the preceding paragraph: [\\u02c6Qmov-null, \\u02c6Qmov-pot] = argmax[Qmov-null ,Qmov-pot ] 1 2 Tr(QT mov-null Cstationary Qmov-null ) \\u2211 dmov-null i=1 \\u03c3stationary (i) + 1 2 Tr(QT mov-pot Cmoving Qmov-pot ) \\u2211 dmov-pot i=1 \\u03c3moving (i) subjectto QT mov-nullQmov-pot =0, QT mov-nullQmov-null = I, QT mov-potQmov-pot = I We quantified the normalized variance explained in a subspace  as in refs. 27,66 (Fig. 5d,k): normVE = Tr (QTCQ) \\u2211 d i=1 \\u03c3 (i) where Q is the subspace; C is the covariance of neural activity; and \\u03c3  are the singular values of C. This normalization provides the maximum  variance that can be captured by d dimensions. Unit activity was reconstructed from movement-null and  movement-potent subspaces according to the following equation: Xsubspace = XQT Xrecon = XsubspaceQ where Xrecon is the reconstructed neural activity; X subspace is the pro - jected neural activity within the movement-null or movement-potent  subspace; X is either single-trial or trial-averaged firing rates; and Q is  either the movement-null or movement-potent subspace. Bootstrapped distributions of CDs within the movement-null and  movement-potent subspaces were obtained through two separate  methods. For Fig. 7 and Extended Data Fig. 7a,c, we first estimated  movement-null and movement-potent subspaces per session and  reconstructed neural activity from each subspace. Then, we performed  the hierarchical bootstrapping procedure as described in the \\u2018Hier - archical bootstrapping\\u2019 subsection. For each iteration, we used the  original neural activity to estimate the CDs and then projected the  reconstructed neural activity onto the CDs. For Extended Data Fig. 5b,  for each bootstrap iteration, the reconstructed neural activity itself  was used to estimate the CDs. Activity wit\\n\\n---\\n\\nPAGE 19\\nNature Neuroscience Article https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-024-01859-1 identified from the reconstructed neural activity, X recon, for the indi- vidual example sessions. Subspace and CD alignment for an individual unit was calculated  as: A = VEmov-null \\u2212VEmov-pot VEmov-null +VEmov-pot where VE is the variance explained of each individual unit by the  movement-null or movement-potent subspace or by the activity along  a CD within the movement-null or movement-potent subspace. VE for  a single unit was calculated as: VE =1 \\u2212 \\u2211t(xt \\u2212 \\u0302xt) 2 \\u2211t(xt \\u2212 x ) 2 where xt is the trial-averaged firing rate; \\u0302x is the trial-averaged firing  rate reconstructed from either a subspace or CD; and t is the time bin. Unit activity was reconstructed from movement-null and  movement-potent CD components according to the following  equation: XCD = X \\u00d7CD T Xrecon = XCD \\u00d7CD where Xrecon is the reconstructed neural activity; X CD is the projected  neural activity within the movement-null or movement-potent CD com- ponents; X is single-trial firing rates; and CD is either the movement-null  or movement-potent CD. T o ask if the number of single units observed to be aligned to either  the movement-null or movement-potent subspace was different than  expected by chance, we compared the distributions to those obtained  from randomly sampled subspaces or CDs using a procedure similar to  the one described in ref. 27. Each element comprising a subspace or CD  was randomly sampled from a normal distribution with zero mean and  unit variance but was biased by the covariance structure of the neural  activity for a given session. Biasing the randomly sampled subspaces  by the covariances controls for the unbalanced variance between  stationary and moving timepoints (or between delay and response  epochs). That is to say that the shuffled distributions take into account  the relative amount of movement tuning across the neural population.  The dimension for each random subspace on each iteration was ran - domly sampled uniformly from the dimensions of the movement-null  and movement-potent subspaces across sessions. The dimension for  each random CD was always 1. We sampled random subspaces or CDs, v mov-null and v mov-pot, as  follows: vmov-null =orth ( Umov-null\\u221aSmov-nullv ||Umov-null\\u221aSmov-nullv||2 ) vmov-pot =orth \\u239b \\u239c\\u239c \\u239d Umov-pot\\u221aSmov-potv ||Umov-pot\\u221aSmov-potv|| 2 \\u239e \\u239f\\u239f \\u23a0 where U and S are the left and right singular vectors of their associated  covariance matrices, Cstationary and Cmoving, and v is a matrix, or vector  in the case of CDs, whose elements are independently drawn from a  normal distribution with zero mean and unit variance. As described  above, covariance matrices Cstationary and Cmoving are defined by neural  activity during stationary and movement timepoints for single-trial  data. orth(A) computes the orthonormal basis of a matrix A . Neural  activity from each session was projected along the randomly sampled  subspaces, and alignment indices were calculated. This procedure was  repeated 1,000 times to generate null distributions, which represents  the alignment indices of our data with random subspaces (Extended  Data Fig. 5). T o generate P values associated with Figs. 5n, 7d,h and 8d,  on each iteration, we computed the proportion of strongly tuned units  (alignment\\u2009\\u2265\\u20090.8 and alignment\\u2009\\u2264\\u2009\\u22120.8). This provided two distribu - tions, one for alignment\\u2009\\u2265\\u20090.8 and another for alignment\\u2009\\u2264\\u2009\\u22120.8. These  chance distributions were separately fit with Gaussian distributions  using the fitgmdist() function in MATLAB. Then, P values were com- puted as the probability of observing the proportion of tuned units  that we observed in the data from the fitted distributions. Subspaces were identified using data from DR and WC correct and  error trials (Figs. 5, 7 and 8). Control analyses were performed using  separate sets of trials to assess if the movement-potent subspace errone- ously contained movement-null-subspace dynamics. First, we estimated  subspaces using WC trials only (Extended Data Figs. 4 and 8). Second,  we estimated subspaces using DR and WC trials, but we restricted  timepoints used to those in the response epoch only (Extended Data  Figs. 4 and 8). Both controls allowed us to estimate movement-null and  movement-potent subspaces in the presence of uninstructed move- ments but in the absence of planning dynamics. These controls thus  allowed us to measure the degree to which the movement-potent sub- space erroneously captured movement-null dynamics. In a further attempt to validate that the movement-potent sub - space is not inadvertently capturing movement-correlated inter - nal dynamics, we identified movement-null and movement-potent  subspaces using a two-stage principal component analysis (PCA)  approach (Extended Data Fig. 4). The movement-null subspace was  first identified as the dominant PCs (first five PCs) of the single-trial  firing rates when mice are not moving. Second, the activity within the  movement-null subspace was removed from single-trial firing rates.  Then, the movement-potent subspace was calculated as the first five  PCs of the single-trial residuals: Xmov-null = XQT mov-null Xrecon,mov-null = Xmov-nullQmov-null Qmov-pot =PCA(X \\u2212 Xrecon,mov-null) Xmov-pot = XQT mov-pot X \\u2208 \\u211d(B\\u00d7K)\\u00d7N is the single-trial firing rates where B is the number of  timepoints; K is the number of trials; and N  is the number of units.  Qmov-null is the first five PCs of the single-trial firing rates when animals  are stationary, and Xmov-null is the projection along those PCs. Xrecon,mov-null  is the reconstructed neural activity obtained from the multiplication  of activity within the movement-null subspace, X mov-null , and the  movement-null subspace, Qmov-null. PCA(Z) indicates computing the PCs  of the matrix Z. Qmov-pot is the first five PCs of the single-trial residuals  obtained from subtracting Xmov-null from the single-trial firing rates, X.  Finally, Xmov-pot is obtained from projecting single-trial firing rates onto  Qmov-pot. Xrecon,mov-null contains activity that is explainable by the first five  PCs in the absence of movement, and, therefore, the residuals contain  movement-related neural dynamics. Thus, performing PCA on these  residuals provides a movement-potent subspace that is orthogonal to  the movement-null subspace. This approach ensures that dynamics  observed during stationarity are contained within the movement-null  subspace before assigning any dynamics to the movement-potent  subspace. Therefore, it is more conservative in avoiding the  mis-assignment of movement-correlated internal dynamics to the  movement-potent subspace.\\n\\n---\\n\\n\"\n3918:            \"input\": \"*** Begin Functions Identified\\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\\n| `loadObjs` | `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/loadObjs.m` | LOADING | Loads each session `.mat` into an `obj` struct and normalizes missing fields (`meta`, `ex`, `me`) so sessions can be concatenated. |\\n| `loadSessionData` | `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/loadSessionData.m` | LOADING | Main session-processing entry point. Iterates over sessions/probes, calls `processData`, and concatenates dual-probe sessions. |\\n| `processData` | `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/processData.m` | PROCESSING | Runs trial selection, cluster selection, spike alignment, PSTH/single-trial extraction, low-FR filtering, and baseline FR summaries. |\\n| `findTrials` | `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/findTrials.m` | CURATION | Evaluates logical trial-condition expressions against `obj.bp` fields and returns trial IDs for each condition. |\\n| `findClusters` | `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/findClusters.m` | CURATION | Selects clusters by quality label; `'all'` excludes `garbage`, `gabrga`, `noisy`, and `real?`. |\\n| `alignSpikes` | `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/alignSpikes.m` | PROCESSING | Aligns each spike time to an event such as `goCue`, `moveOnset`, `firstLick`, `lastLick`, or `jawOnset`. |\\n| `getSeq` | `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/getSeq.m` | PROCESSING | Bins aligned spikes over `tmin:dt:tmax`, smooths them, and creates both condition-averaged PSTHs and single-trial neural arrays. |\\n| `removeLowFRClusters` | `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/removeLowFRClusters.m` | CURATION | Drops units whose mean PSTH firing rate is below the low-FR threshold across conditions. |\\n| `baselineFR` | `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/baselineFR.m` | PROCESSING | Computes baseline firing-rate median and SD in the presample epoch. |\\n| `loadMotionEnergy` | `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/loadMotionEnergy.m` | LOADING | Loads per-trial motion energy, aligns it to the chosen event using video timestamps, interpolates it to the neural time base, and thresholds movement. |\\n| `findVideoOffset` | `/app/code/funcs/findVideoOffset.m` | PROCESSING | Computes the offset between SpikeGLX and video start using bit-code synchronization. |\\n| `findPosition` | `/app/code/funcs/kinematics/findPosition.m` | PROCESSING | Interpolates DLC feature trajectories into the aligned neural time base, with special handling for missing video frames and non-tongue features. |\\n| `findVelocity` | `/app/code/funcs/kinematics/findVelocity.m` | PROCESSING | Computes framewise x/y velocity per tracked feature and uses different missing-data behavior for tongue vs non-tongue features. |\\n| `getKinematicsFromVideo` | `/app/code/funcs/kinematics/getKinematicsFromVideo.m` | PROCESSING | Builds aligned kinematic feature matrices `(time, trial, feature)` from DLC trajectories for both cameras. |\\n| `getKinematics` | `/app/code/funcs/kinematics/getKinematics.m` | PROCESSING | Augments kinematics with tongue angle/length and motion energy, standardizes features, and computes reduced-dimensional video factors. |\\n| `NeuralChoiceDecoding` | `/app/code/ChoiceContextDecoding/NeuralChoiceDecoding.m` | PROCESSING | Example neural decoder setup for lick choice using aligned single-trial neural data. |\\n| `NeuralContextDecoding` | `/app/code/ChoiceContextDecoding/NeuralContextDecoding.m` | PROCESSING | Example neural decoder setup for behavioral context using aligned single-trial neural data. |\\n\\n### Notes\\n- The repository is an electrophysiology + behavior/video pipeline, not calcium imaging, so there is no delta-F/F computation anywhere in the reference loading path.\\n- The canonical neural pipeline is: load `obj` -> select trials with logical expressions over `obj.bp.*` -> select clusters by quality -> align spike times to an event -> bin and smooth spikes -> filter low-FR units.\\n- `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` shows the reference task conditions and a standard alignment to `goCue`, with typical windows `tmin=-2.5`, `tmax=2.5`, and either `dt=1/100` (tutorial example) or `dt=1/200` (`getDefaultParams` default).\\n- The reference code uses smoothed firing rates, not raw spike times, for downstream analyses and decoders.\\n- Default ephys curation in `getDefaultParams.m` uses `quality={'all'}` and `lowFR=0.5` Hz, while the tutorial example in `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` uses `lowFR=1` Hz. This threshold difference needs resolution against the dataset and paper later.\\n- Trial-condition logic in the code explicitly distinguishes delayed-response (`~autowater`) from water-cued (`autowater`) trials and excludes `stim.enable` and `early` trials in the standard conditions.\\n- Video/DLC traces are synchronized to neural time using `findVideoOffset`, then interpolated into the same time axis as neural data relative to the alignment event.\\n- Motion energy is aligned to the same trial-wise event and interpolated to the neural time base in `loadMotionEnergy`.\\n- Missing video is handled explicitly: some trials can be skipped if dropped frames are flagged; non-tongue features are nearest-filled after interpolation, while tongue features retain NaNs longer and are baseline-filled separately for position calculations.\\n- The neural choice/context decoder examples both use neural `obj.trialdat` as input and balanced trial sampling across classes, which is directly relevant to the downstream validation task.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\\n**Status**: COMPLETE\\n\\n### Data Structure\\n- `/app/data` contains four experiment folders:\\n  - `Ephys_Behavior/`: delayed-response and water-cued sessions with electrophysiology, DLC trajectories, and separate `motionEnergy_*.mat` files.\\n  - `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior/`: randomized-delay sessions with electrophysiology for most sessions plus separate `motionEnergy_*.mat` files.\\n  - `DelayInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior/`: behavior/video-only bilateral motor cortex inhibition sessions; motion energy is embedded inside each `data_structure_*.mat`.\\n  - `GoCueInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior/`: behavior/video-only go-cue inhibition sessions; motion energy is embedded inside each `data_structure_*.mat`.\\n- File naming conventions:\\n  - Session files: `data_structure_<animal>_<date>.mat`\\n  - Separate motion energy files for ephys folders: `motionEnergy_<animal>_<date>.mat`\\n- Mixed MATLAB formats are present:\\n  - 109 session files are MATLAB v7.3/HDF5 and can be read with `mat73`/`h5py`.\\n  - 11 session files are older MATLAB format and require `scipy.io.loadmat`.\\n- Native session object layout from representative files:\\n  - Ephys sessions: `obj = {bp, clu, ex, meta, pth, sglx, traj, trials}`\\n  - Behavior-only sessions: `obj = {bp, ex, me, pth, sglx, traj, trials}`\\n- Key raw variables:\\n  - `obj.bp`: trial-level task variables (`L`, `R`, `hit`, `miss`, `no`, `autowater`, `early`, `stim`, `protocol`, `Ntrials`) and event times in `obj.bp.ev` (`sample`, `delay`, `goCue`, `reward`, `lickL`, `lickR`, `bitStart`).\\n  - `obj.clu` (ephys only): per-probe cluster structs with `quality`, `site`, `spkWavs`, `tm`, `trial`, `trialtm`.\\n  - `obj.traj`: two camera views (side and bottom), each storing per-trial `ts`, `frameTimes`, `featNames`, `NdroppedFrames`, and filenames.\\n  - `obj.me`: per-trial motion energy and `moveThresh` for behavior-only sessions.\\n  - Separate `motionEnergy_*.mat`: `me = {data, moveThresh}` for ephys sessions.\\n- Example DLC feature sets observed in ephys sessions:\\n  - View 1 / side camera: `tongue`, `left_tongue`, `right_tongue`, `jaw`, `trident`, `nose`, `lickport`\\n  - View 2 / bottom camera: `top_tongue`, `topleft_tongue`, `bottom_tongue`, `bottomleft_tongue`, `top_paw`, `bottom_paw`, `lickport`, `jaw`, `top_nostril`, `bottom_nostril`\\n- Important edge case found during exploration:\\n  - `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior/data_structure_JEB24_2023-10-03.mat` and `..._2023-10-04.mat` do not contain `obj.clu`; they are behavior-only sessions despite living in an ephys-labeled folder.\\n- Raw session sizes are heterogeneous:\\n  - Ephys sessions range from 17 to 1258 clusters before any quality or firing-rate curation.\\n  - Trial counts range from 209 to 517 across all session files.\\n- Overall raw dataset totals from file inspection:\\n  - 120 session files across 18 mice and 37,073 trials.\\n  - 45 sessions contain neural clusters (14 mice, 15,324 trials, 14,297 raw (total) | Not explicitly reported in text | No explicit trial-count total found in paper text |\\n| Trials / session | Session starts with ~100 DR trials, then alternating 10\\u201325 trial blocks | \\u201capproximately 100 DR trials\\u201d and \\u201cEach interleaved block was 10\\u201325 trials\\u201d |\\n| Neural data time bin | 5 ms for single-trial analyses | \\u201csingle-trial neural activity was first binned in 5-ms intervals\\u201d |\\n| Behavior data time bin | 400 Hz video = 2.5 ms per frame | \\u201cHigh-speed video was captured (400-Hz frame rate)\\u201d |\\n| Reward rate | Not explicitly reported; expert criterion >70% correct | \\u201ctrained \\u2026 until they reached at least 70% accuracy\\u201d |\\n| <Task/behavior statistic 1> | Choice-selective single units in DR: sample 36%, delay 42%, response 58% of 483 single units | \\u201csample: 36%; delay: 42%; response: 58% of 483 single units\\u201d |\\n| <Task/behavior statistic 2> | Context-selective single units: 39% of 214 single units | \\u201c39% of single units, 12 sessions, six mice, 214 single units\\u201d |\\n| ... | Randomized-delay experts: >70% accuracy and <20% early lick rate | \\u201c>70% accuracy and <20% early lick rate\\u201d |\\n\\n\\n### Processing Details\\n- Behavioral paradigm details relevant to conversion:\\n  - DR trials: 1.3 s sample tone, then delay, then 10 ms auditory go cue; ignore trials are no response within 3 s of go cue.\\n  - Fixed-delay DR task: nominal delay 0.9 s for 12 mice and 0.7 s for one mouse, \\u201clinearly time warped to 0.9 s\\u201d.\\n  - Two-context sessions begin with ~100 DR trials and then alternate WC and DR blocks of 10\\u201325 trials; all sessions start with DR.\\n  - WC trials omit auditory cues and present ~3 \\u00b5l water at a random lickport and random time.\\n- Video / behavior processing:\\n  - Two cameras at 400 Hz.\\n  - Tongue, jaw, and nose tracked in both cameras; paws tracked only in the bottom view.\\n  - Missing values are nearest-filled for all tracked features except tongue.\\n  - Velocity is the first derivative of position.\\n  - Motion energy is computed from the absolute difference between medians of the next/previous 5 frames and summarized by the 99th percentile across pixels for each frame.\\n  - Motion-energy movement threshold is set manually per session at the separation between the two modes of a bimodal distribution.\\n- Neural processing:\\n  - Sessions are included only if they have at least 10 units.\\n  - All units with firing rate >1 Hz are used for most analyses.\\n  - Only well-isolated single units with firing rate >1 Hz are used for single-unit selectivity and some subspace-alignment analyses.\\n  - Single-trial neural activity is binned at 5 ms and smoothed with a causal Gaussian kernel with half-width 35 ms.\\n  - Single-trial normalization uses baseline activity from \\u22122.4 s to \\u22122.2 s relative to go cue, during the ITI.\\n- Paper decoder/analysis setup directly relevant to later validation:\\n  - Choice/context decoding used logistic regression with ridge regularization.\\n  - Separate models were trained at each time bin.\\n  - Equal numbers of correct left/right trials were used.\\n  - Four-fold cross-validation was used, with 30% of trials held out for testing.\\n  - Chance performance was estimated by shuffling labels across trials.\\n\\n### Curation Steps\\n\\n**Neuron curation rules**:\\n- Spike sorting used JRCLUST and/or Kilosort 3 with manual curation in Phy 2.\\n- Units were labeled as well-isolated single units based on ISI histogram, separation from other units, and stationarity across the session.\\n- Units passing manual curation but with higher ISI violation rates were treated as multiunits.\\n- Recording sessions were included only if they had at least 10 units.\\n- For most analyses, units had to exceed 1 Hz firing rate.\\n\\n**Trial curation rules**:\\n- Early-lick trials were omitted from analyses.\\n- Ignore trials were omitted from behavioral analyses.\\n- Behavioral-analysis sessions required at least 40 correct DR trials for each direction and 20 correct WC trials for each direction, excluding early and ignore trials.\\n- Logistic choice/context decoders used equalized numbers of correct left/right trials.\\n- Randomized-delay CDchoice fit/test procedure used non-1.2 s delays as fit trials and 1.2 s delays as test trials.\\n\\n### Decoders Trained\\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\\n| Choice from delay-epoch `CDchoice` projection | ROC AUC = 0.86 \\u00b1 0.11 across sessions |\\n| `CDchoice` predicted from movement kinematics (fixed-delay DR) | R\\u00b2 = 0.41 \\u00b1 0.23 across sessions |\\n| `CDramp` predicted from movement kinematics (fixed-delay DR) | R\\u00b2 = 0.46 \\u00b1 0.23 across sessions |\\n| `CDchoice` predicted from movement kinematics (randomized-delay DR) | Average R\\u00b2 = 0.38 \\u00b1 0.20 across sessions |\\n| Choice/context logistic decoding from neural data or kinematics | Time-resolved accuracy curves shown, but no single summary accuracy value stated in the main text |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Step 4: Check for      trim = 0\\n+    smoothed = np.convolve(padded, GAUSSIAN_KERNEL, mode=\\\"same\\\")\\n+    return smoothed[trim:].astype(np.float32)\\n+\\n+\\n+def fill_nearest_1d(values: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    values = np.asarray(values, dtype=np.float64).copy()\\n+    if values.size == 0:\\n+        return values\\n+    valid = np.isfinite(values)\\n+    if not valid.any():\\n+ np.ndarray, new_times: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    times = np.asarray(times, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    values = np.asarray(values, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    valid = np.isfinite(times) & np.isfinite(values)\\n+    if valid.sum() < 2:\\n+        if valid.sum() == 1:\\n+            return np.full(new_times.shape, values[valid][0], dtype=np.float64)\\n+        return np.full(new_times.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    interp = interp1d(\\n+        times[valid],\\n+        values[valid],\\n+        kind=\\\"linear\\\",\\n+        bounds_error=False,\\n+        fill_value=(values[valid][0], values[valid][-1]),\\n+        assume_sorted=False,\\n+    )\\n+    return np.asarray(interp(new_times), dtype=np.float64)\\n+\\n+\\n+def nearest_interp(times: np.ndarray, values: np.ndarray, new_times: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    times = np.asarray(times, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    values = np.asarray(values, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    valid = np.isfinite(times) & np.isfinite(values)\\n+    if valid.sum() == 0:\\n+        return np.full(new_times.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    if valid.sum() == 1:\\n+        return np.full(new_times.shape, values[valid][0], dtype=np.float64)\\n+    interp = interp1d(\\n+        times[valid],\\n+        values[valid],\\n+        kind=\\\"nearest\\\",\\n+        bounds_error=False,\\n+        fill_value=(values[valid][0], values[valid][-1]),\\n+        assume_sorted=False,\\n+    )\\n+    return np.asarray(interp(new_times), dtype=np.float64)\\n+\\n+\\n+def post_go_choice(lick_l: Any, lick_r: Any, go_time: float) -> int:\\n+    left = event_list_after(lick_l, go_time)\\n+    right = event_list_after(lick_r, go_time)\\n+    t_left = left.min() if left.size else np.inf\\n+    t_right = right.min() if right.size else np.inf\\n+    if np.isinf(t_left) and np.isinf(t_right):\\n+        return 2\\n+    return 0 if t_left < t_right else 1\\n+\\n+\\n+def event_list_after(value: Any, threshold: float) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    if value is None:\\n+        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)\\n+    arr = np.array(value, dtype=object).reshape(-1)\\n+    cleaned = []\\n+    for item in arr:\\n+        if item is None:\\n+            continue\\n+        try:\\n+            cleaned.append(float(item))\\n+        except Exception:\\n+            continue\\n+    if not cleaned:\\n+        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)\\n+    cleaned_arr = np.asarray(cleaned, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    return cleaned_arr[cleaned_arr > threshold]\\n+\\n+\\n+def load_motion_energy_source(obj: Any, spec: SessionSpec) -> tuple[list[Any] | None, bool]:\\n+    if spec.motion_path.exists():\\n+        me_struct = None, False\\n+    return motion_energy_data_list(me_struct), True\\n+\\n+\\n+def motion_energy_data_list(me_struct: Any) -> list[Any] | None:\\n+    if me_struct is None:\\n+        return None\\n+ trial_invalid_video(trial_view: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:\\n+    dropped = gget(trial_view, \\\"NdroppedFrames\\\", None)\\n+    if dropped is None:\\n+        return False\\n+    arr = np.asarray(dropped).reshape(-1)\\n+    if arr.size == 0:\\n+        return False\\n+    return np.isnan(arr.astype(float)).all()\\n+\\n+\\n+def trial_frame_times(trial_view: dict[str, Any], n_frames: int) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    frame_times = gget(trial_view, \\\"frameTimes\\\", None)\\n+    if frame_times is None:\\n+        return np.arange(1, n_frames + 1, dtype=np.float64) / 400.0\\n+    frame_times = np.asarray(frame_times, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    if frame_times.size == 0 or np.isnan(frame_times).all():\\n+        return np.arange(1, n_frames + 1, dtype=np.float64) / 400.0\\n+    return frame_times\\n+\\n+\\n+def build_speed_trace(\\n+    obj: Any,\\n+    view_index: int,\\n+    feature_names: list[str],\\n+    go_times: np.ndarray,\\n+    taxis: np.ndarray,\\n+) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, str | None]:\\n+    trials_view = get_trials_view(obj, view_index)\\n+    n_trials = len(go_times)\\n+    speed = np.full((n_trials, taxis.size), np.nan,      break\\n+    if chosen_feature is None:\\n+        return speed, visible, None\\n+\\n+    vidshift = find_video_offset(obj)\\n+    feat_is_tongue = \\\"tongue\\\" in chosen_feature\\n+\\n+    for       coords = ts[:, :2, feat_idx]\\n+        n_frames = coords.shape[0]\\n+        frame_times = trial_frame_times(trial_view, n_frames) - vidshift - go_times[trial_idx]\\n+\\n+        x = coords[:, 0]\\n+        y = coords[:, 1]\\n+        frame_visible = np.isfinite(x) & np.isfinite(y)\\n+        if not frame_visible.any():\\n+            continue\\n+\\n+        x_interp =      x_interp[~vis_interp] = np.nan\\n+        y_interp[~vis_interp] = np.nan\\n+\\n+        if feat_is_tongue:\\n+            x_vel = np.gradient(x_interp)\\n+            y_vel = np.gradient(y_interp)\\n+            x_vel[~np.isfinite(x_vel)] = 0.0\\n+            y_vel[~np.isfinite(y_vel)] = 0.0\\n+        else:\\n+            x_filled = fill_nearest_1d(x_interp)\\n+    x_vel = np.gradient(x_filled) - baseline\\n+            y_vel = np.gradient(y_filled) - baseline\\n+            x_vel = fill_nearest_1d(x_vel)\\n+            y_vel = fill_nearest_1d(y_vel)\\n+\\n+        speed[trial_idx] = np.sqrt(x_vel ** 2 + y_vel ** 2).astype(np.float32)\\n+        visible[trial_idx] = vis_interp\\n+\\n+    return speed, visible, chosen_feature\\n+\\n+\\n+def build_motion_energy_trace(\\n+    obj: Any,\\n+    spec: SessionSpec,\\n+    go_times: np.ndarray,\\n+    taxis: np.ndarray,\\n+) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\\n+    n_trials = len(go_times)\\n+ out[visible_and_high] = 1\\n+    return out, threshold\\n+\\n+\\n+def low_fr_condition_indices(bp: Any, n_trials: int) -> list[np.ndarray]:\\n+    hit = bool_array(bp, \\\"hit\\\", n_trials)\\n+ \\\"not_visible\\\"],\\n+    [\\\"low\\\", \\\"high\\\", \\\"not_visible\\\"],\\n+    [\\\"low\\\", \\\"high\\\", \\\"no_video\\\"],\\n+]\\n+\\n+\\n+@dataclass(frozen=True)\\n+class SessionSpec:\\n+ @property\\n+    def motion_path(self) -> Path:\\n+        return DATA / self.folder / f\\\"motionEnergy_{self.animal}_{self.date}.mat\\\"\\n+\\n+\\n+def main() -> None:\\n+    parser =     \\\"output\\\": [],\\n+        \\\"subjects\\\": [],\\n+        \\\"subject_idx\\\": [],\\n+        \\\"brain_regions\\\": [\\\"ALM\\\"],\\n+        \\\"brain_region_idx\\\": [],\\n+        \\\"input_names\\\": [\\\"time_from_go_cue_s\\\"],\\n+        \\\"output_names\\\": OUTPUT_NAMES,\\n+        \\\"output_values\\\": OUTPUT_VALUES,\\n+        \\\"metadata\\\": {\\n+            \\\"task_description\\\": (\\n+                \\\"ALM electrophysiology aligned to go cue; decode lick direction, \\\"\\n+                \\\"behavioral context, outcome, and discretized \\\"method\\\": \\\"causal_gaussian\\\",\\n+                \\\"window_bins\\\": SMOOTH_BINS,\\n+                \\\"boundary_condition\\\": \\\"reflect\\\",\\n+            },\\n+            \\\"trial_filter\\\": \\\"~stim.enable & ~early & (hit|miss|no)\\\",\\n+            \\\"unit_filter\\\": {\\n+                \\\"quality_excluded\\\": sorted(BAD_QUALITIES),\\n+                \\\"min_mean_firing_rate_hz\\\": LOW_FR,\\n+                \\\"min_units_per_session\\\": MIN_UNITS_PER_SESSION,\\n+            },\\n+            \\\"session_info\\\": [],\\n+        },\\n+    }\\n+\\n+    subject_to_idx: dict[str, int] = {}\\n+\\n+    for sess_num, spec in enumerate(specs, start=1):\\n+        session_t0 = time.perf_counter()\\n+       )\\n+\\n+    converted[\\\"subjects\\\"] = [subject for subject, _ in sorted(subject_to_idx.items(), key=lambda kv: kv[1])]\\n+    converted[\\\"subject_idx\\\"] = marker=\\\"randmeta\\\")\\n+\\n+    specs: list[SessionSpec] = []\\n+    for animal in fixed_animals:\\n+        specs.extend(parse_loader_sessions(animal, cohort=\\\"fixed\\\",   first_randomized = next(spec for spec in specs if spec.cohort == \\\"randomized\\\")\\n+    return [first_fixed, first_randomized]\\n+\\n+\\n+def parse_loader_animals(script_path: Path, marker: str) -> list[str]:\\n+    text = script_path.read_text()\\n+    animals: list[str] = []\\n+    for line in text.splitlines():\\n+        line = line.strip()\\n+        if line.startswith(\\\"%\\\"):\\n+            continue\\n+        match = re.search(rf\\\"{re.escape(marker)}\\\\s*=\\\\s*load([A-Za-z0-9]+)_ALMVideo\\\\(\\\", line)\\n+        if match:\\n+            animals.append(match.group(1))\\n+    return animals\\n+\\n+\\n+def parse_loader_sessions(animal: str, cohort: str, folder: str) -> list[SessionSpec]:\\n+    text = (LOADER_DIR / f\\\"load{animal}_ALMVideo.m\\\").read_text()\\n+    sessions: list[SessionSpec] = []\\n+    current_date: str | None = None\\n+    for raw_line in text.splitlines():\\n+        line = raw_line.strip()\\n+        if line.startswith(\\\"%\\\"):\\n+            continue\\n+        m_date = re.search(r\\\"date = '([^']+)'\\\", line)\\n+        if m_date:\\n+            current_date = m_date.group(1)\\n+        m_probe = re.search(r\\\"probe = (\\\\[[^\\\\]]+\\\\]|\\\\d+);\\\", line)\\n+        if m_probe and current_date:\\n+            probes = tuple(int(x) for x in re.findall(r\\\"\\\\d+\\\", m_probe.group(1)))\\n+            sessions.append(SessionSpec(cohort=cohort, folder=folder, animal=animal, date=current_date, probes=probes))\\n+            current_date = None\\n+    return sessions\\n+\\n+\\n+def load_mat_file(path: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:\\n+    try:\\n+        return mat73.loadmat(str(path))\\n+    except Exception:\\n+        return scipy.io.loadmat(str(path), simplify_cells=True)\\n+\\n+\\n+def gget(obj: Any, key: str, default: Any = None) -> Any:\\n+    if isinstance(obj, dict):\\n+        return obj.get(key, default)\\n+    return getattr(obj, key, default)\\n+\\n+\\n+def as_list(value: Any) -> list[Any]:\\n+    if value is None:\\n+        return []\\n+    if isinstance(value, list):\\n+        return value\\n+    if isinstance(value, tuple):\\n+        return list(value)\\n+    if isinstance(value, np.ndarray):\\n+        if value.dtype == object:\\n+            return list(value.ravel())\\n+        return list(value)\\n+    return [value]\\n+\\n+\\n+def normalize_string(value: Any) -> str:\\n+    if value is None:\\n+        return \\\"\\\"\\n+    return str(value).strip()\\n+\\n+\\n+def flatten_dicts(value: Any) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\\n+    if isinstance(value, dict):\\n+        return [value]\\n+    if isinstance(value, list):\\n+        out: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\\n+        for item in value:\\n+            out.extend(flatten_dicts(item))\\n+        return out\\n+    if isinstance(value, np.ndarray) and value.dtype == object:\\n+        out: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\\n+        for item in value.ravel():\\n+            out.extend(flatten_dicts(item))\\n+        return out\\n+    return []\\n+\\n+\\n+def is_flat_unit_list(clu: Any) -> bool:\\n+    return (\\n+        isinstance(clu, list)\\n+        and len(clu) > 0\\n+        and isinstance(clu[0], dict)\\n+        and \\\"tm\\\" in clu[0]\\n+        and \\\"quality\\\" in clu[0]\\n+        and not isinstance(gget(clu[0], \\\"quality\\\"), (list, np.ndarray))\\n+    )\\n+\\n+\\n+def split_probe_struct(probe: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\\n+    qualities = as_list(gget(probe, \\\"quality\\\"))\\n+    tm_list = as_list(gget(probe, \\\"tm\\\"))\\n+    trial_list = as_list(gget(probe, \\\"trial\\\"))\\n+    trialtm_list = as_list(gget(probe, \\\"trialtm\\\"))\\n+    site_list = as_list(gget(probe, \\\"site\\\", [None] * len(qualities)))\\n+    n_units = len(qualities)\\n+\\n+    units: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\\n+    for i in range(n_units):\\n+        units.append(\\n+            {\\n+                \\\"quality\\\": qualities[i] if i < len(qualities) else \\\"\\\",\\n+                \\\"tm\\\": np.asarray(tm_list[i]).reshape(-1) if i < len(tm_list) else np.array([], dtype=float),\\n+                \\\"trial\\\": np.asarray(trial_list[i]).reshape(-1) if i < len(trial_list) else np.array([], dtype=float),\\n+                \\\"trialtm\\\": (\\n+                    np.asarray(trialtm_list[i]).reshape(-1) if i < len(trialtm_list) else np.array([], dtype=float)\\n+                ),\\n+                \\\"site\\\": site_list[i] if i < len(site_list) else None,\\n+            }\\n+        )\\n+    return units\\n+\\n+\\n+def extract_selected_units(obj: Any, probes: tuple[int, ...]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\\n+    clu = gget(obj, \\\"clu\\\")\\n+    if clu is isinstance(clu, dict):\\n+        units = split_probe_struct(clu)\\n+        return [unit for unit in units if normalize_string(unit[\\\"quality\\\"]).lower() not in BAD_QUALITIES]\\n+\\n+    clu_list = as_list(clu)\\n+    units: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\\n+    for probe_num in probes:\\n+        probe = clu_list[probe_num - 1]\\n+        for unit in split_probe_struct(probe):\\n+            if normalize_string(unit[\\\"quality\\\"]).lower() in BAD_QUALITIES:\\n+                continue\\n+            units.append(unit)\\n+    return units\\n+\\n+\\n+def bool_array(bp: Any, field: str, n_trials: int) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    values = gget(bp, field, None)\\n+    if values is None:\\n+        return np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=bool)\\n+    values = np.asarray(values).reshape(-1)\\n+    return values.astype(bool)\\n+\\n+\\n+def stim_enable_array(bp: Any, n_trials: int) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    stim = gget(bp, \\\"stim\\\", None)\\n+    if stim is None:\\n+        return arr.astype(float)\\n+\\n+\\n+def get_trials_view(obj: Any, view_index: int) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\\n+    traj = gget(obj, \\\"traj\\\")\\n+    if traj is None:\\n+        return []\\n+    view = isinstance(value, list):\\n+        out: list[str] = []\\n+        for item in value:\\n+            out.extend(flatten_strings(item))\\n+        return out\\n+    if isinstance(value, np.ndarray):\\n+        if value.dtype == object:\\n+            out: list[str] = []\\n+            for item in value.ravel():\\n+                out.extend(flatten_strings(item))\\n+            return out\\n+        return [str(x) for x in value.ravel()]\\n+    return [str(value)]\\n+\\n+\\n+def feature_index(trial_view: dict[str, Any], feature_name: str) -> int | None:\\n+    feat_names = matlab_mode(values: np.ndarray) -> float:\\n+    values = np.asarray(values).reshape(-1)\\n+    values = values[np.isfinite(values)]\\n+    if values.size == 0:\\n+        return 0.0\\n+    rounded = np.round(values, 9)\\n+    unique, counts = np.unique(rounded, return_counts=True)\\n+    return float(unique[np.argmax(counts)])\\n+\\n+\\n+def find_video_offset(obj: Any) -> float:\\n+    bp = None or bitcode is None or fs in (None, 0):\\n+        return 0.0\\n+    bit_start = event_array(ev, \\\"bitStart\\\")\\n+    bitcode_start = np.asarray(gget(bitcode, \\\"bitstart\\\")).reshape(-1).astype(float)\\n+    return matlab_mode(bitcode_start) / float(fs) - matlab_mode(bit_start)\\n+\\n+\\n+def make_causal_kernel(window_bins: int) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    if window_bins <= 1:\\n+        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)\\n+    std = (window_bins - 1) / (2.0 * 2.5)\\n+    kernel = gaussian(window_bins, std=std).astype(np.float64)\\n+    kernel[: window_bins // 2] = 0.0\\n+    kernel /= kernel.sum()\\n+    return kernel\\n+\\n+\\n+GAUSSIAN_KERNEL = make_causal_kernel(SMOOTH_BINS)\\n+\\n+\\n+def my_smooth_1d(values: np.ndarray, boundary_condition: str = \\\"reflect\\\") -> np.ndarray:\\n+    values = np.asarray(values, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    if values.size == 0 or SMOOTH_BINS <= 1:\\n+        return values.astype(np.float32)\\n+    if boundary_condition == \\\"reflect\\\":\\n+        padded = np.concatenate([values[:SMOOTH_BINS], values])\\n+        trim = SMOOTH_BINS\\n+    elif boundary_condition == \\\"zeropad\\\":\\n+        padded = np.concatenate([np.zeros(SMOOTH_BINS, dtype=np.float64), values])\\n+        trim = SMOOTH_BINS\\n+    else:\\n+        padded = values\\n+        trim = 0\\n+    smoothed = np.convolve(padded, GAUSSIAN_KERNEL, mode=\\\"same\\\")\\n+    return smoothed[trim:].astype(np.float32)\\n+\\n+\\n+def fill_nearest_1d(values: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    values = np.asarray(values, dtype=np.float64).copy()\\n+    if values.size == 0:\\n+        return values\\n+    valid = np.isfinite(values)\\n+    if not valid.any():\\n+        return values\\n+    idx = np.arange(values.size)\\n+    values[~valid] = np.interp(idx[~valid], idx[valid], values[valid])\\n+    return values\\n+\\n+\\n+def linear_interp(times: np.ndarray, values: np.ndarray, new_times: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    times = np.asarray(times, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    values = np.asarray(values, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    valid = np.isfinite(times) & np.isfinite(values)\\n+    if valid.sum() < 2:\\n+        if valid.sum() == 1:\\n+            return np.full(new_times.shape, values[valid][0], dtype=np.float64)\\n+        return np.full(new_times.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    interp = interp1d(\\n+        times[valid],\\n+        values[valid],\\n+        kind=\\\"linear\\\",\\n+        bounds_error=False,\\n+        fill_value=(values[valid][0], values[valid][-1]),\\n+        assume_sorted=False,\\n+    )\\n+    return np.asarray(interp(new_times), dtype=np.float64)\\n+\\n+\\n+def nearest_interp(times: np.ndarray, values: np.ndarray, new_times: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    times = np.asarray(times, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    values = np.asarray(values, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    valid = np.isfinite(times) & np.isfinite(values)\\n+    if valid.sum() == 0:\\n+        return np.full(new_times.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    if valid.sum() == 1:\\n+        return np.full(new_times.shape, values[valid][0], dtype=np.float64)\\n+    interp = interp1d(\\n+        times[valid],\\n+        values[valid],\\n+        kind=\\\"nearest\\\",\\n+        bounds_error=False,\\n+        fill_value=(values[valid][0], values[valid][-1]),\\n+        assume_sorted=False,\\n+    )\\n+    return np.asarray(interp(new_times), dtype=np.float64)\\n+\\n+\\n+def post_go_choice(lick_l: Any, lick_r: Any, go_time: float) -> int:\\n+    left = event_list_after(lick_l, go_time)\\n+    right = event_list_after(lick_r, go_time)\\n+    t_left = left.min() if left.size else np.inf\\n+    t_right = right.min() if right.size else np.inf\\n+    if np.isinf(t_left) and np.isinf(t_right):\\n+        return 2\\n+    return 0 if t_left < t_right else 1\\n+\\n+\\n+def event_list_after(value: Any, threshold: float) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    if value is None:\\n+        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)\\n+    arr = np.array(value, dtype=object).reshape(-1)\\n+    cleaned = []\\n+    for item in arr:\\n+        if item is None:\\n+            continue\\n+        try:\\n+            cleaned.append(float(item))\\n+        except Exception:\\n+            continue\\n+    if not cleaned:\\n+        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)\\n+    cleaned_arr = np.asarray(cleaned, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    return cleaned_arr[cleaned_arr > threshold]\\n+\\n+\\n+def load_motion_energy_source(obj: Any, spec: SessionSpec) -> tuple[list[Any] | None, bool]:\\n+    if spec.motion_path.exists():\\n+        me_struct = None, False\\n+    return motion_energy_data_list(me_struct), True\\n+\\n+\\n+def motion_energy_data_list(me_struct: Any) -> list[Any] | None:\\n+    if me_struct is None:\\n+        return None\\n+ np.asarray(dropped).reshape(-1)\\n+    if arr.size == 0:\\n+        return False\\n+    return np.isnan(arr.astype(float)).all()\\n+\\n+\\n+def trial_frame_times(trial_view: dict[str, Any], n_frames: frame_times = np.asarray(frame_times, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    if frame_times.size == 0 or np.isnan(frame_times).all():\\n+        return np.arange(1, n_frames + 1, dtype=np.float64) / 400.0\\n+    return frame_times\\n+\\n+\\n+def build_speed_trace(\\n+    obj: Any,\\n+    view_index: int,\\n+    feature_names: list[str],\\n+    go_times: np.ndarray,\\n+    taxis: np.ndarray,\\n+) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, str | None]:\\n+    trials_view = get_trials_view(obj, view_index)\\n+    n_trials = len(go_times)\\n+    speed = np.full((n_trials, taxis.size), np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\\n+    visible = np.zeros((n_trials, taxis.size), dtype=bool)\\n+    chosen_feature: str | None = None\\n+\\n+    if len(trials_view) < n_trials:\\n+        return speed, visible, delta-F/F computation anywhere in the reference loading path.\\n- The canonical neural pipeline is: load `obj` -> select trials with logical expressions over `obj.bp.*` -> select clusters by quality -> align spike times to an event -> bin and smooth spikes -> filter low-FR units.\\n- `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` shows the reference task conditions and a standard alignment to `goCue`, with typical windows `tmin=-2.5`, `tmax=2.5`, and either `dt=1/100` (tutorial example) or `dt=1/200` (`getDefaultParams` default).\\n- The reference code uses smoothed firing rates, not raw spike times, for downstream analyses and decoders.\\n- Default ephys curation in `getDefaultParams.m` uses `quality={'all'}` and `lowFR=0.5` Hz, while the tutorial example in `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` uses `lowFR=1` Hz. This threshold difference needs resolution against the dataset and paper later.\\n- Trial-condition logic in the code explicitly distinguishes delayed-response (`~autowater`) from water-cued (`autowater`) trials and excludes `stim.enable` and `early` trials in the standard conditions.\\n- Video/DLC traces are synchronized to neural time using `findVideoOffset`, then interpolated into the same time axis as neural data relative to the alignment event.\\n- Motion energy is aligned to the same trial-wise event and interpolated to the neural time base in `loadMotionEnergy`.\\n- Missing video is handled explicitly: some trials can be skipped if dropped frames are flagged; non-tongue features are nearest-filled after interpolation, while tongue features retain NaNs longer and are baseline-filled separately for position calculations.\\n- The neural choice/context decoder examples both use neural `obj.trialdat` as input and balanced trial sampling across classes, which is directly relevant to the downstream validation task.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\\n**Status**: COMPLETE\\n\\n### Data Structure\\n- `/app/data` contains four experiment folders:\\n  - activity was first binned in 5-ms intervals\\u201d |\\n| Behavior data time bin | 400 Hz video = 2.5 ms per frame | \\u201cHigh-speed video was captured (400-Hz frame rate)\\u201d |\\n| Reward rate | Not explicitly reported; expert criterion >70% correct | \\u201ctrained \\u2026 until they reached at least 70% accuracy\\u201d |\\n| <Task/behavior statistic 1> | Choice-selective single units in DR: sample 36%, delay 42%, response 58% of 483 single units | \\u201csample: 36%; delay: 42%; response: 58% of 483 single units\\u201d |\\n| <Task/behavior statistic 2> | Context-selective single units: 39% of 214 single units | \\u201c39% of single units, 12 sessions, six mice, 214 single units\\u201d |\\n| ... | Randomized-delay experts: >70% accuracy and <20% early lick rate | \\u201c>70% accuracy and <20% early lick rate\\u201d |\\n\\n\\n### Processing Details\\n- Behavioral paradigm details relevant to conversion:\\n  - DR trials: 1.3 s sample tone, then delay, then 10 ms auditory go cue; ignore trials are no response within 3 s of go cue.\\n  - Fixed-delay DR task: nominal delay 0.9 s for 12 mice and 0.7 s for one mouse, \\u201clinearly time warped to 0.9 s\\u201d.\\n  - Two-context sessions begin with ~100 DR trials and then alternate WC and DR blocks of 10\\u201325 trials; all sessions start with DR.\\n  - WC trials omit auditory cues and present ~3 \\u00b5l water at a random lickport and random time.\\n- Video / behavior processing:\\n  - Two cameras at 400 Hz.\\n  - Tongue, jaw, and nose tracked in both cameras; paws tracked only in the bottom view.\\n  - Missing values are nearest-filled for all tracked features except tongue.\\n  - Velocity is the first derivative of position.\\n  - Motion energy is computed from the absolute difference between medians of the next/previous 5 frames and summarized by the 99th percentile across pixels for each frame.\\n  - Motion-energy movement threshold is set manually per session at the separation between the two modes of a bimodal distribution.\\n- Neural processing:\\n  - Sessions are included only if they have at least 10 units.\\n  - All units with firing rate >1 Hz are used for most analyses.\\n  - Only well-isolated single units with firing rate >1 Hz are used for single-unit selectivity and some subspace-alignment analyses.\\n  - Single-trial neural activity is binned at 5 ms and smoothed with a causal Gaussian kernel with half-width 35 ms.\\n  - Single-trial normalization uses baseline activity from \\u22122.4 s to \\u22122.2 s relative to go cue, during the ITI.\\n- Paper decoder/analysis setup directly relevant to later validation:\\n  - Choice/context decoding used logistic regression with ridge regularization.\\n  - Separate models were trained at each time bin.\\n  - Equal numbers of correct left/right trials were used.\\n  - Four-fold cross-validation was used, with 30% of trials held out for testing.\\n  - Chance performance was estimated by shuffling labels across trials.\\n\\n### Curation Steps\\n\\n**Neuron curation rules**:\\n- Spike sorting used JRCLUST and/or Kilosort 3 with manual curation in Phy 2.\\n- Units were labeled as well-isolated single units based on ISI histogram, separation from other units, and stationarity across the session.\\n- Units passing manual curation but with higher ISI violation rates were treated as multiunits.\\n- Recording sessions were included only if they had at least 10 units.\\n- For most analyses, units had to exceed 1 Hz firing rate.\\n\\n**Trial curation rules**:\\n- Early-lick trials were omitted from analyses.\\n- Ignore trials were omitted from behavioral analyses.\\n- Behavioral-analysis sessions required at least 40 correct DR trials for each direction and 20 correct WC trials for each direction, excluding early and ignore trials.\\n- Logistic choice/context decoders used equalized numbers of correct left/right trials.\\n- Randomized-delay CDchoice fit/test procedure used non-1.2 s delays as fit trials and 1.2 s delays as test trials.\\n\\n### Decoders Trained\\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\\n| Choice from delay-epoch `CDchoice` projection | ROC AUC = 0.86 \\u00b1 0.11 across sessions |\\n| `CDchoice` predicted from movement kinematics (fixed-delay DR) | R\\u00b2 = 0.41 \\u00b1 0.23 across sessions |\\n| `CDramp` predicted from movement kinematics (fixed-delay DR) | R\\u00b2 = 0.46 \\u00b1 0.23 across sessions |\\n| `CDchoice` predicted from movement kinematics (randomized-delay DR) | Average R\\u00b2 = 0.38 \\u00b1 0.20 across sessions |\\n| Choice/context logistic decoding from neural data or kinematics | Time-resolved accuracy curves shown, but no single summary accuracy value stated in the main text |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\\n**Status**: COMPLETE\\n\\n### Discrepancies Found\\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\\n| Randomized-delay session count | `Figure3h.m` / `Figure3i.m` load exactly 19 sessions from `JEB11`, `JEB12`, `JEB23`, `JEB24` via the `loadJEB*_ALMVideo.m` scripts. `loadJEB23_ALMVideo.m` comments out `2023-10-20`, and `loadJEB24_ALMVideo.m` starts at `2023-10-23`. | The raw folder contains 22 session files, including `JEB24` `2023-10-03` and `2023-10-04` with no `obj.clu`, plus `JEB23` `2023-10-20`. | Randomized-delay cohort reported as 19 sessions from 4 mice. | Mirror the loader-defined 19-session cohort, not the raw folder count. Exclude the two behavior-only `JEB24` sessions and the commented-out `JEB23` `2023-10-20` session. |\\n| Fixed-delay and context mouse counts | `Figure3h.m` fixed-delay loaders enumerate 25 sessions across 10 animal IDs (`JEB6`, `JEB7`, `EKH1`, `EKH3`, `JGR2`, `JGR3`, `JEB13`, `JEB14`, `JEB15`, `JEB19`). `Figure8a_thru_c.m` context loaders enumerate 12 sessions across 7 animal IDs (`JEB6`, `JEB7`, `EKH1`, `EKH3`, `JGR2`, `JGR3`, `JEB19`). | Raw file names match the loader animal IDs. | Paper text states 25 sessions from 9 mice and 12 sessions from 6 mice. | Follow the explicit loader session lists from the released code. Treat the paper mouse counts as an apparent manuscript/counting inconsistency unless later evidence shows that two IDs belong to the same animal. |\\n| Raw neuron counts vs paper neuron counts | Reference loaders select specific probes per session, including mixed single-probe and dual-probe sessions (for example `JEB15` uses `[1 2]` on three dates). `findClusters('all')` excludes only `garbage`, `gabrga`, `noisy`, and `real?`. | Counting all raw clusters across every probe overestimates the paper-comparable totals. Using loader-selected probes and `findClusters('all')` logic gives 1,566 fixed-delay units and 948 randomized-delay units before any firing-rate/session inclusion filter. | Paper reports 1,651 fixed-delay units and 845 randomized-delay units. | Final conversion must use the loader-selected probes, then apply the reference low-FR and session-inclusion rules before comparing counts. The remaining mismatch is small enough to attribute to downstream firing-rate/session filtering and mixed file layouts rather than a cohort-definition error. |\\n| `obj.clu` storage layout differs by file format | MATLAB code assumes `obj.clu{probe}` for most HDF5/v7.3 files. | Newer v7.3 sessions usually store `obj.clu` as a list of probe structs with `quality` vectors, but older MATLAB-format sessions (for example randomized `JEB23` `2023-10-18`, `JEB23` `2023-10-21`, and all loader-included `JEB24` sessions) store `obj.clu` as a flat list of unit dicts for a single probe. | Paper does not discuss this implementation detail. | The Python loader must branch on `obj.clu` layout: detect probe-structured vs flat-unit storage and normalize both into one per-session unit table before filtering/alignment. |\\n| Alignment event on WC trials | `Figure8a_thru_c.m` sets `params.alignEvent = 'goCue'` even when mixing DR and WC trials. | In raw context sessions, WC trials still have valid `bp.ev.goCue` values; median `goCue-sample` is 2.2 s and median `goCue-delay` is 0.9 s on both WC and DR trials. Reward occurs later on WC than on DR. | Context analyses are reported relative to go cue / response timing. | Align all trials to `goCue` exactly as requested by the decoder task and as done in the released context-analysis code. Do not substitute reward time for WC trials. |\\n| Availability of WC trials outside the 12-session context cohort | Figure 8 uses only the 12-session subset loaded from `JEB6`, `JEB7`, `EKH1`, `EKH3`, `JGR2`, `JGR3`, `JEB19`. | Additional fixed-delay ephys sessions from `JEB13`, `JEB14`, and `JEB15` also contain nonzero `autowater` trial counts, though often fewer than the canonical context sessions. | Paper highlights a dedicated \\u201ctwo-context\\u201d cohort of 12 sessions. | Treat `bp.autowater` as the authoritative trial-wise context label. The 12-session context cohort is a stricter analysis subset, not evidence that other sessions lack context information. |\\n\\n### Final Understanding\\n- The defensible neural cohort for conversion is the union of the released ephys loader cohorts, interpreted through the loader scripts rather than the raw folder counts.\\n- Session inclusion must respect the per-session probe choice encoded in the `load*_ALMVideo.m` scripts.\\n- Mixed MATLAB file layouts are a real data-loading edge case and must be handled explicitly in Python.\\n- Trial-wise context should come directly from `bp.autowater`, while the paper\\u2019s 12-session context cohort should be treated as a specialized subset used for one analysis figure.\\n- Go-cue alignment is consistent across the decoder task, the reference figure scripts, and the raw event tables, including WC trials.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\\n**Status**: COMPLETE\\n\\n### Variable Mapping\\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\\n| `obj.clu` selected by per-session loader probe(s) | `neural` | Load only the probe(s) specified by the released `load*_ALMVideo.m` scripts, exclude cluster qualities `garbage`, `gabrga`, `noisy`, `real?`, align spike times to `bp.ev.goCue`, bin on a common `dt=0.01 s` grid over `[-2.5, 2.5] s`, smooth with causal `mySmooth(...,15,'reflect')`, then remove units with mean FR `< 1 Hz` across conditions. Save each trial as `(n_neurons, n_timepoints)` float32 firing rates in Hz. | `loadSessionData`, `processData`, `findClusters`, `alignSpikes`, `getSeq`, `removeLowFRClusters`, `mySmooth` | This follows the figure-level decoder/kinematic scripts (`Figure3h.m`, `Figure8a_thru_c.m`) more closely than the generic defaults in `getDefaultParams.m`. |\\n| `obj.time` after neural binning | `input[0]` | Use the neural time vector relative to go cue, repeated identically for every trial in the session as a `(1, T)` float32 array. | `getSeq` | Decoder task specifies time from go cue onset as the only decoder input. |\\n| `bp.ev.lickL`, `bp.ev.lickR`, `bp.ev.goCue` | `output[0]` (`lick_direction`) | Find the earliest lick event after go cue. Encode `left=0`, `right=1`, `none=2`, then broadcast across all `T` bins within the trial. | `firstLickTime` (timing logic), raw lick-event arrays | Uses actual first response side, not instructed side. Miss trials therefore flip relative to `bp.L`/`bp.R` as expected. |\\n| `bp.autowater` | `output[1]` (`behavioral_context`) | Encode `WC=0` when `autowater==1`, `DR=1` when `autowater==0`, then broadcast across time. | Trial-condition logic in `findTrials`; context conditions in `Figure8a_thru_c.m` | Use `autowater` directly even outside the 12-session context-analysis subset. |\\n| `bp.hit`, `bp.miss`, `bp.no` | `output[2]` (`outcome`) | Encode `incorrect=0` for miss, `correct=1` for hit, `ignore=2` for no-response, then broadcast across time. | `getOutcome`, standard trial fields in `bp` | Keep ignore trials because the decoder task explicitly requires them, even though some behavioral analyses omitted them. |\\n| Side-view tongue trajectory (`tongue`; fallback `left_tongue` / `right_tongue`) | `output[3]` (`tongue_velocity`) | Reuse the reference interpolation/alignment logic from `findPosition`/`findVelocity`, but retain the pre-fill visibility mask. Compute tongue speed magnitude from aligned x/y velocity. Within each session, threshold visible samples at the 50th percentile: `<p50 -> 0`, `>=p50 -> 1`, `not visible -> 2`. | `getKinematicsFromVideo`, `findPosition`, `findVelocity` | The reference code sets missing tongue velocity to 0 after interpolation; for the decoder task we preserve a separate \\u201cnot visible\\u201d class before that fill step. |\\n| Bottom-view paw trajectory (`top_paw`; fallback `bottom_paw`) | `output[4]` (`paw_velocity`) | Use the same interpolation/alignment logic as the reference video code, compute speed magnitude from aligned x/y velocity, threshold visible samples at the session 50th percentile, and encode `not visible -> 2`. | `Figure1e.m` feature choice, `findPosition`, `findVelocity` | The shared code explicitly analyzes `top_paw_yvel_view2`; using `top_paw` as the primary paw marker is the closest published precedent. |\\n| Motion energy file `me.data` / `obj.me.data` and aligned video timing | `output[5]` (`motion_energy`) | Load and align motion energy on the same neural time base as `loadMotionEnergy`. On trials with usable video, threshold aligned motion-energy samples at the session 50th percentile: `<p50 -> 0`, `>=p50 -> 1`. If the trial/session lacks usable video or motion-energy data, encode `2` for the affected bins. | `loadMotionEnergy`, `findVideoOffset` | This intentionally differs from the paper\\u2019s manual bimodal movement threshold because the decoder task explicitly requests a per-session median split. |\\n\\n### Key Decisions\\n1. **Use the loader-defined neural cohort, not every raw file**: The released paper code defines the paper-comparable cohorts explicitly through the `load*_ALMVideo.m` scripts. The conversion will therefore use the union of the fixed-delay and randomized-delay ephys loader sessions (44 sessions total before downstream filtering), rather than every raw `.mat` file with neural data.\\n2. **Exclude `stim.enable` and `early` trials, but keep hit/miss/no trials**: Reference trial definitions routinely exclude perturbation/stimulation and early-lick trials. Ignore/no-response trials are retained because the decoder task requires the `ignore` outcome class.\\n3. **Use one common alignment event and one common bin size for all sessions**: Align everything to `goCue`, use `dt=0.01 s`, and use a common window `[-2.5, 2.5] s`. This matches the released figure scripts and tutorial path closely while avoiding unnecessary extrapolation at very early times.\\n4. **Store outputs as fully time-varying `(doutput, T)` arrays**: Although lick direction, context, and outcome are trial-level variables, they will be repeated across time bins so they can coexist with time-varying kinematic outputs in a single consistent output array per trial.\\n5. **Keep neural activity as smoothed firing rates, not z-scored residuals**: The reference loading path and decoder examples operate directly on `obj.trialdat` after spike alignment/binning/smoothing. Additional z-scoring or subspace projection would make the saved dataset less general and less faithful to the primary loader output.\\n6. **Extend the reference kinematic extraction only where the decoder task forces it**: The shared scripts already define the correct video synchronization, interpolation, smoothing, and missing-data handling. The only deliberate extensions are: adding a paw feature to the requested trajectory list, computing scalar speed magnitudes, and preserving missing-visibility masks for the required `not visible` / `no video` classes.\\n7. **Use trial-event licks for lick direction instead of `bp.L` / `bp.R`**: `bp.L` and `bp.R` encode the trial side, not the animal\\u2019s realized first post-go-cue lick. Actual lick direction should come from `bp.ev.lickL` and `bp.ev.lickR`.\\n8. **Require at least 10 retained units per session and at least 2 valid trials per saved session**: This matches the paper\\u2019s session-inclusion rule and the decoder\\u2019s minimum practical requirement.\\n9. **Represent all neurons as ALM**: The loader names and `getDefaultParams.m` indicate the recorded area is ALM for these ephys/video cohorts, so `brain_regions = ['ALM']` is sufficient unless a raw-data contradiction appears during implementation.\\n\\n### Planned Sanity Checks\\n- [ ] Recompute one trial-by-trial neural firing-rate vector from raw spike times with `np.histogram` + causal Gaussian smoothing and confirm it matches the saved neural array with `np.allclose()`.\\n- [ ] Recompute one aligned motion-energy trace directly from raw `me.data`, `frameTimes`, `findVideoOffset`, and `goCue`, then compare against the saved motion-energy-derived output bins.\\n- [ ] Recompute one tongue-speed trace and one paw-speed trace directly from raw DLC trajectories and confirm the saved visible/not-visible masks and discretized bins match.\\n- [ ] Spot-check three trials where lick direction is obvious from `lickL` / `lickR` timestamps and verify agreement with saved `lick_direction`, `context`, and `outcome`.\\n- [ ] Check that the saved session/unit counts after filtering are consistent with the loader-selected cohorts and with the paper\\u2019s approximate neuron/session totals.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Step 6: Script Development\\n**Status**: COMPLETE\\n\\n[Implementation notes]\\n- Implemented `/app/convert_data.py` with the required CLI:\\n  - `python -u /app/convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`\\n  - `--full` / `--sample`\\n  - `--show-processing`\\n- The script now:\\n  - Parses the released figure/loader scripts to recover the paper-defined session lists and per-session probe selections.\\n  - Loads both MATLAB v7.3/HDF5 and older MATLAB files.\\n  - Normalizes the two observed `obj.clu` layouts:\\n    - per-probe structs with vectorized fields\\n    - flat per-unit lists in older single-probe files\\n  - Normalizes the observed `obj.traj` layouts, including the v7.3 case where each camera view is a dict of trial-indexed fields.\\n  - Reimplements the reference spike pipeline in Python:\\n    - go-cue alignment\\n    - 10 ms binning over `[-2.5, 2.5] s`\\n    - causal Gaussian smoothing matching `mySmooth(...,15,'reflect')`\\n    - quality filtering\\n    - low-FR filtering\\n  - Builds trial-wise outputs for lick direction, context, outcome, tongue velocity, paw velocity, and motion energy on the common neural time base.\\n  - Emits optional per-session processing plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\\n\\nCode inefficiencies identified:\\n- MATLAB-style nested structs/cells produce expensive Python-side branching if normalized repeatedly inside inner loops.\\n- Video alignment can become expensive if feature-name parsing or trajectory-layout parsing happens per trial instead of once per session.\\n\\nCode speedups added:\\n- Session lists and probe selections are parsed once from the reference loader scripts, not rediscovered from the filesystem every session.\\n- Trajectory and cluster layouts are normalized once per session before inner-loop processing.\\n- Neural trial tensors are preallocated per session and filled in place.\\n- The sample run indicates roughly 5-6 seconds per session on two representative sessions, which is short enough that no parallelization is required yet.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\\n**Status**: COMPLETE\\n\\n### Sample Statistics\\n\"\n10704:            \"content\": \"Command: /bin/bash -lc \\\"nl -ba /app/convert_data.py | sed -n '1,260p'\\\"\\nChunk ID: c4f4f4\\nWall time: 0.0000 seconds\\nProcess exited with code 0\\nOriginal token count: 2559\\nOutput:\\n     1\\t#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n     2\\t\\\"\\\"\\\"Convert the Birnbaum et al. `10 ms`\\n+\\n+## Main Files\\n+- `/app/converted_data.pkl`: full converted dataset\\n+- `/app/sample_data.pkl`: 2-session sample conversion\\n+- `/app/convert_data.py`: conversion script\\n+-
